````math
Substituting $`A_{l}=A`$, $`B_{l}=B`$, $`C_{l}=C`$, and $`\xi_{l}=0`$
into
Theorem [B.7](#A2.Thmtheorem7 "Theorem B.7 (Realistic Aggregate SNR Bound). ‣ B.4 Realistic Aggregate Signal-to-Noise Ratio Bound ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
and simplifying the resulting geometric series gives the result. ∎

###### Corollary B.9 (Ideal Edge-Wise Gating as a Special Case).

Suppose there exist constants $`g_{+}>g_{-}>0`$, $`\alpha_{+}>0`$,
$`\alpha_{-}>0`$, $`g_{0}\geq 0`$, and $`\lambda_{\mathrm{leak}}\geq 0`$
such that the effective retention inside the evidence region is
$`A=g_{+}\alpha_{+}`$, the self-propagation inside the noise region is
$`B=g_{-}\alpha_{-}`$, the evidence-to-noise leakage is
$`C=g_{0}\lambda_{\mathrm{leak}}`$, and $`\xi_{l}=0`$. Then
Theorem [B.7](#A2.Thmtheorem7 "Theorem B.7 (Realistic Aggregate SNR Bound). ‣ B.4 Realistic Aggregate Signal-to-Noise Ratio Bound ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
reduces to

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\operatorname{SNR}_{L}\geq\left[\left(\frac{g_{-}\alpha_{-}}{g_{+}\alpha_{+}}\right)^{L}\operatorname{SNR}_{0}^{-1}+\frac{g_{0}\lambda_{\mathrm{leak}}}{g_{+}\alpha_{+}}\sum_{i=0}^{L-1}\left(\frac{g_{-}\alpha_{-}}{g_{+}\alpha_{+}}\right)^{i}\right]^{-1}.
``` |  | (44) |

If $`\lambda_{\mathrm{leak}}=0`$, then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\operatorname{SNR}_{L}\geq\left(\frac{g_{+}\alpha_{+}}{g_{-}\alpha_{-}}\right)^{L}\operatorname{SNR}_{0}.
``` |  | (45) |

### B.5 Document Retrieval Budget

The final retrieval targets of the SAGE-GFM reader are memory fragments
or documents. Therefore, we need to convert the entity-level SNR bound
into a document-level top-$`k`$ budget bound. Let the final document
score be $`S_{D}(d)`$, and let the top-$`k`$ retrieval result be

|     |                                                    |     |      |
|-----|----------------------------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       P_{k}(q,G)=\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k_{d\in D}S_{D}(d).
       ```                                                 |     | (46) |

###### Definition B.10 ($`\rho`$-Coverage Retrieval Budget).

Given $`0<\rho\leq\rho_{A}`$, let

|     |                                   |     |      |
|-----|-----------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       m_{\rho}=\lceil\rho|D^{+}|\rceil.
       ```                                |     | (47) |

The minimum top-$`k`$ budget required to achieve $`\rho`$-level gold
evidence coverage is defined as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(q,G)=\min\left\{k:\ |P_{k}(q,G)\cap D^{+}|\geq m_{\rho}\right\}.
``` |  | (48) |

Let $`\tau_{\rho}^{+}`$ denote the $`m_{\rho}`$-th largest score among
gold evidence documents, namely the gold score threshold required to
achieve $`\rho`$-coverage.

###### Lemma B.11 (Quantile Retrieval Budget Bound).

Let the total score mass of distractor documents be

|     |                                                 |     |      |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       M_{L}^{-}=\sum_{d\in D\setminus D^{+}}S_{D}(d).
       ```                                              |     | (49) |

If $`\tau_{\rho}^{+}>0`$, then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(q,G)\leq m_{\rho}+\frac{M_{L}^{-}}{\tau_{\rho}^{+}}.
``` |  | (50) |

###### Proof.

Define the set of distractor documents whose scores are not lower than
$`\tau_{\rho}^{+}`$ as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{N}_{\rho}=\{d\in D\setminus D^{+}:S_{D}(d)\geq\tau_{\rho}^{+}\}.
``` |  | (51) |

For any $`d\in\mathcal{N}_{\rho}`$, we have
$`S_{D}(d)\geq\tau_{\rho}^{+}`$, and hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
|\mathcal{N}_{\rho}|\tau_{\rho}^{+}\leq\sum_{d\in\mathcal{N}_{\rho}}S_{D}(d)\leq M_{L}^{-}.
``` |  | (52) |

Thus $`|\mathcal{N}_{\rho}|\leq M_{L}^{-}/\tau_{\rho}^{+}`$. To ensure
that the top-$`k`$ results contain at least $`m_{\rho}`$ gold evidence
documents, it suffices to include these $`m_{\rho}`$ gold documents and
all distractors whose scores are not lower than the threshold
$`\tau_{\rho}^{+}`$. Therefore,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(q,G)\leq m_{\rho}+|\mathcal{N}_{\rho}|\leq m_{\rho}+\frac{M_{L}^{-}}{\tau_{\rho}^{+}}.
``` |  | (53) |

This proves the lemma. ∎

To use entity-level SNR for document-level retrieval, we need to control
the noise expansion introduced by entity-to-document projection.

###### Assumption B.12 (Projection Noise and Gold Score Concentration).

There exist constants $`K_{A}\geq 0`$, $`\zeta_{A}\geq 0`$, and
$`c_{\rho}\in(0,1]`$ such that the final document scores satisfy

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle M_{L}^{-}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq K_{A}N_{L}+\zeta_{A},`$ |  | (54) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\tau_{\rho}^{+}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\rho}}{m_{\rho}}S_{L}.`$ |  | (55) |

Here, $`K_{A}`$ is the noise expansion factor of entity-to-document
projection, $`\zeta_{A}`$ denotes the projection residual caused by
incorrect anchors, missing anchors, or additional text-similarity terms,
and $`c_{\rho}`$ measures whether the evidence signal is effectively
distributed over at least $`m_{\rho}`$ gold documents.

###### Theorem B.13 (Realistic Signal–Noise–Budget Bound).

Under the conditions of
Theorem [B.7](#A2.Thmtheorem7 "Theorem B.7 (Realistic Aggregate SNR Bound). ‣ B.4 Realistic Aggregate Signal-to-Noise Ratio Bound ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"),
further assume that
Assumption [B.12](#A2.Thmtheorem12 "Assumption B.12 (Projection Noise and Gold Score Concentration). ‣ B.5 Document Retrieval Budget ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
holds. Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(q,G)\leq m_{\rho}+\frac{m_{\rho}K_{A}}{c_{\rho}}\operatorname{SNR}_{L}^{-1}+\frac{m_{\rho}\zeta_{A}}{c_{\rho}S_{L}}.
``` |  | (56) |

Substituting
Theorem [B.7](#A2.Thmtheorem7 "Theorem B.7 (Realistic Aggregate SNR Bound). ‣ B.4 Realistic Aggregate Signal-to-Noise Ratio Bound ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
further yields the explicit upper bound

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(q,G)\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle m_{\rho}+\frac{m_{\rho}K_{A}}{c_{\rho}}\Bigg[\left(\prod_{l=1}^{L}\frac{B_{l}}{A_{l}}\right)\operatorname{SNR}_{0}^{-1}`$ |  | (57) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{L}\left(\frac{C_{i}}{A_{i}}+\frac{\xi_{i}}{A_{i}S_{i-1}}\right)\prod_{t=i+1}^{L}\frac{B_{t}}{A_{t}}\Bigg]+\frac{m_{\rho}\zeta_{A}}{c_{\rho}S_{L}}.`$ |  |  |

###### Proof.

By
Lemma [B.11](#A2.Thmtheorem11 "Lemma B.11 (Quantile Retrieval Budget Bound). ‣ B.5 Document Retrieval Budget ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"),

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(q,G)\leq m_{\rho}+\frac{M_{L}^{-}}{\tau_{\rho}^{+}}.
``` |  | (58) |

By
Assumption [B.12](#A2.Thmtheorem12 "Assumption B.12 (Projection Noise and Gold Score Concentration). ‣ B.5 Document Retrieval Budget ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"),

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{M_{L}^{-}}{\tau_{\rho}^{+}}\leq\frac{K_{A}N_{L}+\zeta_{A}}{(c_{\rho}/m_{\rho})S_{L}}=\frac{m_{\rho}K_{A}}{c_{\rho}}\frac{N_{L}}{S_{L}}+\frac{m_{\rho}\zeta_{A}}{c_{\rho}S_{L}}.
``` |  | (59) |

Since $`N_{L}/S_{L}=\operatorname{SNR}_{L}^{-1}`$,
Eq. ([56](#A2.E56 "Equation 56 ‣ Theorem B.13 (Realistic Signal–Noise–Budget Bound). ‣ B.5 Document Retrieval Budget ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
follows. Substituting the upper bound on $`\operatorname{SNR}_{L}^{-1}`$
from
Theorem [B.7](#A2.Thmtheorem7 "Theorem B.7 (Realistic Aggregate SNR Bound). ‣ B.4 Realistic Aggregate Signal-to-Noise Ratio Bound ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
into
Eq. ([56](#A2.E56 "Equation 56 ‣ Theorem B.13 (Realistic Signal–Noise–Budget Bound). ‣ B.5 Document Retrieval Budget ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
gives
Eq. ([57](#A2.E57 "Equation 57 ‣ Theorem B.13 (Realistic Signal–Noise–Budget Bound). ‣ B.5 Document Retrieval Budget ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")).
∎

###### Corollary B.14 (Full Evidence Recovery Budget).

If $`\rho=1`$ and $`\rho_{A}=1`$, then $`m_{\rho}=|D^{+}|`$, and
$`\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(q,G)`$ reduces to the full evidence recovery
budget. In this case,
Theorem [B.13](#A2.Thmtheorem13 "Theorem B.13 (Realistic Signal–Noise–Budget Bound). ‣ B.5 Document Retrieval Budget ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
provides an upper bound on the top-$`k`$ budget required to recover all
gold evidence.

### B.6 Interpretation of the Theoretical Bound for the SAGE Design

Theorem [B.7](#A2.Thmtheorem7 "Theorem B.7 (Realistic Aggregate SNR Bound). ‣ B.4 Realistic Aggregate Signal-to-Noise Ratio Bound ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
and
Theorem [B.13](#A2.Thmtheorem13 "Theorem B.13 (Realistic Signal–Noise–Budget Bound). ‣ B.5 Document Retrieval Budget ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
unify four reader design factors under the same retrieval-budget upper
bound. To avoid relying only on intuitive discussion, we provide several
direct monotonicity propositions.

###### Proposition 2 (Monotonicity of the Budget Bound).

Fix $`m_{\rho}`$, $`K_{A}`$, $`c_{\rho}`$, $`\zeta_{A}`$, $`S_{L}`$, and
$`\operatorname{SNR}_{0}`$, and define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Gamma_{L}=\left(\prod_{l=1}^{L}\frac{B_{l}}{A_{l}}\right)\operatorname{SNR}_{0}^{-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{L}\left(\frac{C_{i}}{A_{i}}+\frac{\xi_{i}}{A_{i}S_{i-1}}\right)\prod_{t=i+1}^{L}\frac{B_{t}}{A_{t}}.
``` |  | (60) |

Then the budget upper bound

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
U_{\rho}=m_{\rho}+\frac{m_{\rho}K_{A}}{c_{\rho}}\Gamma_{L}+\frac{m_{\rho}\zeta_{A}}{c_{\rho}S_{L}}
``` |  | (61) |

is monotonically nondecreasing in $`\Gamma_{L}`$, $`K_{A}`$, and
$`\zeta_{A}`$, and monotonically nonincreasing in $`c_{\rho}`$ and
$`S_{L}`$. If all other terms in the products are fixed, decreasing any
of $`B_{l}/A_{l}`$, $`C_{l}/A_{l}`$, or $`\xi_{l}/(A_{l}S_{l-1})`$
cannot increase $`U_{\rho}`$.

###### Proof.

The partial derivatives of $`U_{\rho}`$ with respect to $`\Gamma_{L}`$,
$`K_{A}`$, and $`\zeta_{A}`$ are nonnegative, while the partial
derivatives with respect to $`c_{\rho}`$ and $`S_{L}`$ are nonpositive.
Moreover, $`\Gamma_{L}`$ is a nonnegative linear or multiplicative
combination of $`B_{l}/A_{l}`$, $`C_{l}/A_{l}`$, and
$`\xi_{l}/(A_{l}S_{l-1})`$. When the other terms are fixed, decreasing
any such nonnegative term cannot increase $`\Gamma_{L}`$. The
proposition follows. ∎

###### Proposition 3 (Effect of Soft Addressing).

If soft addressing increases the initial evidence signal $`S_{0}`$ and
decreases the initial noise mass $`N_{0}`$, thereby increasing
$`\operatorname{SNR}_{0}`$, then, with all other coefficients fixed, the
budget upper bound in
Theorem [B.13](#A2.Thmtheorem13 "Theorem B.13 (Realistic Signal–Noise–Budget Bound). ‣ B.5 Document Retrieval Budget ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
does not increase. In particular, explicit entities, aliases,
pseudo-queries, type constraints, hard constraints, and entity-linking
signals in query planning improve the final budget bound whenever they
increase $`S_{0}/N_{0}`$ in an aggregate sense.

###### Proposition 4 (Aggregate Advantage of Structural Gating).

Compared with a reader without structural gating, suppose the
structurally gated reader satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{B_{l}^{\mathrm{gate}}}{A_{l}^{\mathrm{gate}}}\leq\frac{B_{l}^{\mathrm{plain}}}{A_{l}^{\mathrm{plain}}},\qquad\frac{C_{l}^{\mathrm{gate}}}{A_{l}^{\mathrm{gate}}}\leq\frac{C_{l}^{\mathrm{plain}}}{A_{l}^{\mathrm{plain}}},\qquad\frac{\xi_{l}^{\mathrm{gate}}}{A_{l}^{\mathrm{gate}}S_{l-1}^{\mathrm{gate}}}\leq\frac{\xi_{l}^{\mathrm{plain}}}{A_{l}^{\mathrm{plain}}S_{l-1}^{\mathrm{plain}}}.
``` |  | (62) |

Then the budget upper bound of the structurally gated reader is no
larger than that of the ungated reader.

###### Proposition 5 (Stability Interpretation of the Context–Schema Dual Channel).

Let $`\delta_{l}`$ denote the failure probability of the aggregate
recurrences in
Eqs. ([28](#A2.E28 "Equation 28 ‣ Lemma B.6 (Aggregate Propagation Recurrence). ‣ B.3 Aggregate Propagation Assumptions and Structural Gating Coefficients ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))–([29](#A2.E29 "Equation 29 ‣ Lemma B.6 (Aggregate Propagation Recurrence). ‣ B.3 Aggregate Propagation Assumptions and Structural Gating Coefficients ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
at layer $`l`$. If the schema prior channel reduces the variance of
cross-graph structural-role estimation and the context calibration
channel reduces the current-graph adaptation error, so that
$`\delta_{l}`$ decreases to $`\delta_{l}^{\prime}`$ with
$`\delta_{l}^{\prime}\leq\delta_{l}`$, then the probability lower bound
under which
Theorem [B.7](#A2.Thmtheorem7 "Theorem B.7 (Realistic Aggregate SNR Bound). ‣ B.4 Realistic Aggregate Signal-to-Noise Ratio Bound ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
and
Theorem [B.13](#A2.Thmtheorem13 "Theorem B.13 (Realistic Signal–Noise–Budget Bound). ‣ B.5 Document Retrieval Budget ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
simultaneously hold improves from $`1-\sum_{l=1}^{L}\delta_{l}`$ to
$`1-\sum_{l=1}^{L}\delta_{l}^{\prime}`$.

The core quantities derived above are

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\operatorname{SNR}_{L}^{-1}\leq\left(\prod_{l=1}^{L}\frac{B_{l}}{A_{l}}\right)\operatorname{SNR}_{0}^{-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{L}\left(\frac{C_{i}}{A_{i}}+\frac{\xi_{i}}{A_{i}S_{i-1}}\right)\prod_{t=i+1}^{L}\frac{B_{t}}{A_{t}},
``` |  | (63) |

and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(q,G)\leq m_{\rho}+\frac{m_{\rho}K_{A}}{c_{\rho}}\operatorname{SNR}_{L}^{-1}+\frac{m_{\rho}\zeta_{A}}{c_{\rho}S_{L}}.
``` |  | (64) |

Equation ([63](#A2.E63 "Equation 63 ‣ B.6 Interpretation of the Theoretical Bound for the SAGE Design ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
shows that soft addressing reduces the amount of noise that subsequent
propagation must overcome by improving the initial
$`\operatorname{SNR}_{0}`$; structural gating improves aggregate
evidence retention and noise suppression by increasing $`A_{l}`$ and
decreasing $`B_{l}`$ and $`C_{l}`$; the context–schema dual channel
makes these aggregate inequalities more stable on dynamic graph memories
by reducing cross-graph structural-role estimation error; and
entity-to-document projection converts entity-level SNR into
document-level budget efficiency by decreasing $`K_{A}`$ and
$`\zeta_{A}`$ and increasing $`c_{\rho}`$.
Equation ([64](#A2.E64 "Equation 64 ‣ B.6 Interpretation of the Theoretical Bound for the SAGE Design ‣ Appendix B Signal-to-Noise Ratio and Retrieval Budget of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
further shows that the advantage of SAGE-GFM does not rely on perfect
edge-wise classification or zero-leakage assumptions. As long as
evidence-retention dominance is achieved in an aggregate or
high-probability sense, i.e., $`B_{l}/A_{l}`$ and $`C_{l}/A_{l}`$ are
sufficiently small, the reader can improve query-relevant SNR and reduce
the top-$`k`$ retrieval budget required to achieve a given level of
evidence coverage.

## Appendix C Target Graph Calibration and Cross-graph Structural Priors

### C.1 Structural Role Decomposition Assumption

###### Definition C.1 (Structural role mapping).

Given a graph $`G`$, a mapping

|     |                             |     |      |
|-----|-----------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \rho_{G}:V(G)\to\mathcal{R}
       ```                          |     | (65) |

is called a structural role mapping, where $`\mathcal{R}`$ is the
structural role space. $`\rho_{G}(v)`$ can be jointly determined by
$`\varphi_{G}(v)`$, the structural statistics of edges incident to
$`v`$, local community-boundary statistics, and other graph-structure
summaries. Typical structural roles include hub, bridge, community core,
boundary node, and noisy shortcut.

###### Definition C.2 (Target graph reading risk).

Fix a target graph $`G`$. Let $`\mathcal{D}_{G}`$ be the query–node
sampling distribution on the target graph, and let
$`f_{G}^{\star}(q,v)`$ be the ideal evidence relevance function. For any
measurable function $`f`$, define the squared risk as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{R}_{G}(f)=\mathbb{E}_{(q,v)\sim\mathcal{D}_{G}}\left[\left(f(q,v,G)-f_{G}^{\star}(q,v)\right)^{2}\right].
``` |  | (66) |

###### Assumption C.3 (Context–schema decomposability).

For every target graph $`G`$, the ideal reading function can be
decomposed as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
f_{G}^{\star}(q,v)=f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star}(q,\rho_{G}(v))+f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star}(q,v),
``` |  | (67) |

where $`f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star}`$ denotes the cross-graph shared
structural reading rule, and $`f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star}`$ denotes the
target-graph residual induced by the current writer, current domain,
current entity naming, local noise, and writing style.

Equation
([67](#A3.E67 "Equation 67 ‣ Assumption C.3 (Context–schema decomposability). ‣ C.1 Structural Role Decomposition Assumption ‣ Appendix C Target Graph Calibration and Cross-graph Structural Priors ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
corresponds exactly to the structural design of SAGE:
$`H_{\mathrm{sch}}`$ is used to approximate
$`f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star}`$, and $`H_{\mathrm{ctx}}`$ is used to
approximate $`f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star}`$. The next subsection gives
the risk meaning of this decomposition.

### C.2 Approximation Risk of Context–schema Decomposition

###### Theorem C.4 (Context–schema decomposition reduces target-graph approximation risk).

Suppose Assumption 2.3 holds. Let $`\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$ be the
schema function class, let $`\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ctx},G}`$ be the
target-graph context function class, and define the sum class

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}+\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ctx},G}=\{f_{s}+f_{c}:f_{s}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}},f_{c}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ctx},G}\}.
``` |  | (68) |

If there exist $`\epsilon_{\mathrm{sch}},\epsilon_{\mathrm{ctx}}\geq 0`$
such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\inf_{f_{s}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}}\mathbb{E}\left[(f_{s}(q,\rho_{G}(v))-f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star}(q,\rho_{G}(v)))^{2}\right]\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{sch}},
``` |  | (69) |

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\inf_{f_{c}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ctx},G}}\mathbb{E}\left[(f_{c}(q,v,G)-f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star}(q,v))^{2}\right]\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{ctx}},
``` |  | (70) |

where both expectations are over $`(q,v)\sim\mathcal{D}_{G}`$, then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}+\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ctx},G}}\mathcal{R}_{G}(f)\leq 2\epsilon_{\mathrm{sch}}+2\epsilon_{\mathrm{ctx}}.
``` |  | (71) |

###### Proof.

Take any $`\alpha>0`$. By the two approximation error conditions, there
exist $`\hat{f}_{s}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$ and
$`\hat{f}_{c}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ctx},G}`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[(\hat{f}_{s}-f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star})^{2}]\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{sch}}+\alpha,\qquad\mathbb{E}[(\hat{f}_{c}-f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star})^{2}]\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{ctx}}+\alpha.
``` |  | (72) |

Let $`\hat{f}=\hat{f}_{s}+\hat{f}_{c}`$. By the decomposition in
Eq. ([67](#A3.E67 "Equation 67 ‣ Assumption C.3 (Context–schema decomposability). ‣ C.1 Structural Role Decomposition Assumption ‣ Appendix C Target Graph Calibration and Cross-graph Structural Priors ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\hat{f}-f_{G}^{\star}=(\hat{f}_{s}-f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star})+(\hat{f}_{c}-f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star}).
``` |  | (73) |

Using $`(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{G}(\hat{f})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\hat{f}-f_{G}^{\star})^{2}]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}[(\hat{f}_{s}-f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star})^{2}]+2\mathbb{E}[(\hat{f}_{c}-f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star})^{2}]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon_{\mathrm{sch}}+2\epsilon_{\mathrm{ctx}}+4\alpha.`$ |  | (74) |

Since $`\alpha>0`$ is arbitrary, taking the infimum yields the
conclusion. ∎

###### Proposition 6 (Residual bias of schema-only models).

Further assume that $`L_{2}(\mathcal{D}_{G})`$ is a Hilbert space,
$`\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$ is a closed linear subspace of it, and
$`f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$. If only a
schema-only model $`f_{s}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$ is used, then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\inf_{f_{s}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}}\mathcal{R}_{G}(f_{s})=\dist_{L_{2}(\mathcal{D}_{G})}^{2}(f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star},\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}).
``` |  | (75) |

Therefore, as long as the target-graph residual
$`f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star}`$ does not belong to
$`\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$, a schema-only reader has an irreducible
target-graph bias.

###### Proof.

By $`f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$ and the
linearity of $`\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$, any
$`f_{s}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$ can be written as
$`f_{s}=f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star}+g`$, where
$`g\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$. Thus,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{G}(f_{s})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\left\lVert f_{s}-f_{\mathrm{sch}}^{\star}-f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(\mathcal{D}_{G})}^{2}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\left\lVert g-f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star}\right\rVert_{L_{2}(\mathcal{D}_{G})}^{2}.`$ |  | (76) |

Taking the infimum over $`f_{s}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$ is
equivalent to taking the infimum over
$`g\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{sch}}`$, and
Eq. ([75](#A3.E75 "Equation 75 ‣ Proposition 6 (Residual bias of schema-only models). ‣ C.2 Approximation Risk of Context–schema Decomposition ‣ Appendix C Target Graph Calibration and Cross-graph Structural Priors ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
follows from the definition of distance. ∎

###### Remark C.5.

Proposition 2.5 shows that the cross-graph schema prior can only
characterize cross-graph shared structural rules and cannot replace
target graph calibration. In contrast, the role of the target graph
calibration channel $`H_{\mathrm{ctx}}`$ is to absorb
$`f_{\mathrm{ctx},G}^{\star}`$, namely the local noise, entity
granularity, relation style, and domain residual of the graph generated
by the current writer.

### C.3 Sample Complexity Advantage of Schema Prior

###### Lemma C.6 (Uniform convergence for bounded loss classes).

Let $`\mathcal{F}`$ be a function class, and let the loss
$`\ell_{f}(z)\in[0,1]`$. Given $`n`$ independent samples
$`S=\{z_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}`$, define the true risk
$`R(f)=\mathbb{E}[\ell_{f}(z)]`$ and the empirical risk
$`\widehat{R}_{S}(f)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{f}(z_{i})`$. Then, with
probability at least $`1-\delta`$, for all $`f\in\mathcal{F}`$
simultaneously,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
R(f)\leq\widehat{R}_{S}(f)+2\Rad_{n}(\ell\circ\mathcal{F})+3\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}},
``` |  | (77) |

where $`\Rad_{n}(\ell\circ\mathcal{F})`$ is an upper bound on the
empirical Rademacher complexity of the loss-composed class.

###### Proof.

This is a direct result of the standard symmetrization and
McDiarmid/Hoeffding concentration inequalities for bounded loss function
classes. Specifically, let

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
Z(S)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left\lvert R(f)-\widehat{R}_{S}(f)\right\rvert.
``` |  | (78) |

By symmetrization,
$`\mathbb{E}Z(S)\leq 2\Rad_{n}(\ell\circ\mathcal{F})`$. Moreover, since
changing one sample can change $`Z(S)`$ by at most $`1/n`$, McDiarmid’s
inequality gives

|     |                                                          |     |      |
|-----|----------------------------------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       Z(S)\leq\mathbb{E}Z(S)+3\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}}
       ```                                                       |     | (79) |

with probability at least $`1-\delta`$. Combining the two inequalities
gives the conclusion. ∎

###### Theorem C.7 (Schema prior reduces the sample complexity of target-graph adaptation).

Fix a target graph $`G`$, and suppose the number of supervised samples
available for reader calibration on the target graph is $`n_{G}`$. Let
$`\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{full}}`$ be the full reader class that needs to
be learned on the target graph when no schema prior is used; let
$`\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{res}}`$ be the residual class that only needs to
be learned given a schema prior $`f_{s}`$, with the combined model
$`f=f_{s}+f_{r}`$, $`f_{r}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{res}}`$. Let
$`\hat{f}_{\mathrm{full}}`$ and $`\hat{f}_{\mathrm{res}}`$ be the
empirical risk minimizers over the two classes, respectively. Then, with
probability at least $`1-\delta`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{G}(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{full}})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{full}}}\mathcal{R}_{G}(f)+4\Rad_{n_{G}}(\ell\circ\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{full}})+6\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{2n_{G}}},`$ |  | (80) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{G}(f_{s}+\hat{f}_{\mathrm{res}})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\inf_{f_{r}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{res}}}\mathcal{R}_{G}(f_{s}+f_{r})+4\Rad_{n_{G}}(\ell\circ(f_{s}+\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{res}}))+6\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{2n_{G}}}.`$ |  | (81) |

If the complexities satisfy

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Rad_{n_{G}}(\ell\circ\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{full}})=\widetilde{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d_{\mathrm{full}}}{n_{G}}}\right),\qquad\Rad_{n_{G}}(\ell\circ(f_{s}+\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{res}}))=\widetilde{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d_{\mathrm{res}}}{n_{G}}}\right),
``` |  | (82) |

and $`d_{\mathrm{res}}\ll d_{\mathrm{full}}`$, then the schema prior
reduces target-graph learning from estimating the full reading function
to estimating the residual, and lowers the estimation error term for
target-graph adaptation.

###### Proof.

For any function class $`\mathcal{F}`$, let $`\hat{f}`$ be the empirical
risk minimizer and let $`f^{\circ}\in\arg\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}}R(f)`$ be
the true risk minimizer. By empirical optimality,
$`\widehat{R}(\hat{f})\leq\widehat{R}(f^{\circ})`$, so

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle R(\hat{f})-R(f^{\circ})`$ | $`\displaystyle=R(\hat{f})-\widehat{R}(\hat{f})+\widehat{R}(\hat{f})-\widehat{R}(f^{\circ})+\widehat{R}(f^{\circ})-R(f^{\circ})`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left\lvert R(f)-\widehat{R}(f)\right\rvert.`$ |  | (83) |

Applying Lemma 2.7 to $`\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{full}}`$ and
$`\mathcal{F}=f_{s}+\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{res}}`$, respectively, and
combining the two probability events by a union bound, gives
Eq. ([80](#A3.E80 "Equation 80 ‣ Theorem C.7 (Schema prior reduces the sample complexity of target-graph adaptation). ‣ C.3 Sample Complexity Advantage of Schema Prior ‣ Appendix C Target Graph Calibration and Cross-graph Structural Priors ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
and
Eq. ([81](#A3.E81 "Equation 81 ‣ Theorem C.7 (Schema prior reduces the sample complexity of target-graph adaptation). ‣ C.3 Sample Complexity Advantage of Schema Prior ‣ Appendix C Target Graph Calibration and Cross-graph Structural Priors ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")).
The complexity-order conclusion follows by substituting the
corresponding Rademacher upper bounds. ∎

###### Remark C.8.

Theorem 2.8 gives the key theoretical motivation for the schema prior:
in self-evolving memory, each new graph generated by the writer usually
provides only limited supervised signal. If the reader relearns the full
reading function from the target graph in every round, high-variance
estimation arises; the schema prior fixes or strongly regularizes the
cross-graph shared structural rules, so that target-graph calibration
only needs to learn the residual, thereby reducing sample complexity.

## Appendix D Writer-induced Graph Distribution Shift and Target Graph Calibration

### D.1 Writer-induced Dynamic Graph Distribution

The writer parameter $`\theta`$ induces a graph distribution
$`P_{\theta}(G\mid q,D)`$ on the sample $`(q,D)`$. For notational
simplicity, denote the joint distribution of $`(q,D,D^{+},y,G)`$ by
$`\Pi_{\theta}`$. Given the reader parameter $`\phi`$, define the reader
risk as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}_{R}(\phi;\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{(q,D,D^{+},y,G)\sim\Pi_{\theta}}\left[\ell_{R}(R_{\phi}(q,G,D),D^{+},y)\right].
``` |  | (84) |

Here, $`\ell_{R}`$ can be supporting-entity BCE, multi-positive ranking
loss, document recall loss, or a combination thereof.

###### Proposition 7 (Writer updates cause reader distribution shift).

Assume $`0\leq\ell_{R}\leq 1`$. For any fixed $`\phi`$ and any writer
parameters $`\theta,\theta^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lvert\mathcal{L}_{R}(\phi;\theta^{\prime})-\mathcal{L}_{R}(\phi;\theta)\right\rvert\leq\TV(\Pi_{\theta^{\prime}},\Pi_{\theta}),
``` |  | (85) |

where $`\TV`$ is the total variation distance. If, further,
$`\ell_{R}(R_{\phi}(q,G,D),D^{+},y)`$ is $`L_{\ell}`$-Lipschitz with
respect to the graph variable under some graph metric
$`d_{\mathcal{G}}`$, then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lvert\mathcal{L}_{R}(\phi;\theta^{\prime})-\mathcal{L}_{R}(\phi;\theta)\right\rvert\leq L_{\ell}W_{1}(\Pi_{\theta^{\prime}},\Pi_{\theta}),
``` |  | (86) |

where $`W_{1}`$ is the first-order Wasserstein distance induced by
$`d_{\mathcal{G}}`$.

###### Proof.

For the bounded loss case, let
$`h_{\phi}(q,D,D^{+},y,G)=\ell_{R}(R_{\phi}(q,G,D),D^{+},y)\in[0,1]`$.
Then

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{L}_{R}(\phi;\theta^{\prime})-\mathcal{L}_{R}(\phi;\theta)\right\rvert`$ | $`\displaystyle=\left\lvert\int h_{\phi}\,d\Pi_{\theta^{\prime}}-\int h_{\phi}\,d\Pi_{\theta}\right\rvert\leq\TV(\Pi_{\theta^{\prime}},\Pi_{\theta}),`$ |  | (87) |

where the last step follows from the dual definition of total variation.
If $`h_{\phi}`$ is $`L_{\ell}`$-Lipschitz, the Wasserstein upper bound
follows from the Kantorovich–Rubinstein duality. ∎

###### Corollary D.1 (Necessity of target graph calibration).

If updating the writer from $`\theta`$ to $`\theta^{\prime}`$ causes
$`\TV(\Pi_{\theta^{\prime}},\Pi_{\theta})`$ to be non-negligible, then
the risk of a fixed reader $`\phi`$ on the new graph distribution may
increase. Therefore, target graph calibration of the reader, namely
$`\phi\mapsto\phi^{\prime}`$ to reduce
$`\mathcal{L}_{R}(\phi^{\prime};\theta^{\prime})`$, is a necessary
mechanism for handling writer-induced graph distribution shift.

###### Proof.

By Proposition 3.1, writer distribution shift can directly change the
new-distribution risk of the fixed reader. If the reader is not updated,
there is no optimization mechanism to offset this drift term. Target
graph calibration is precisely re-optimization of
$`\mathcal{L}_{R}(\cdot;\theta^{\prime})`$, and is therefore a natural
step for reducing the risk on the new graph. ∎

## Appendix E Reader Stability under Dynamic Graph Evolution

### E.1 Realistic Graph Evolution Distance

Real graphs often contain hubs, node additions and deletions, alias
merges, anchor rewrites, and structural statistics that are not globally
Lipschitz. Therefore, we use the augmented graph drift actually
perceived by the reader to measure graph evolution.

###### Definition E.1 (Padding alignment and presence bit).

Given two consecutive-round graphs $`G`$ and $`G^{\prime}`$, align them
through persistent memory ids to a common node universe
$`\bar{V}=V(G)\cup V(G^{\prime})`$. If a node exists only in one graph,
it is treated as an isolated padding node in the other graph, and a
presence bit is added to its features. The aligned node feature matrices
are still denoted by $`X,X^{\prime}`$.

###### Definition E.2 (Augmented graph drift).

Let $`A,A^{\prime}`$ be self-looped row-normalized adjacency matrices,
let $`S_{q},S_{q}^{\prime}`$ be the entry score vectors before soft
addressing, and let $`B,B^{\prime}`$ be row-normalized
entity-to-document anchoring matrices. Define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta_{X}=\left\lVert X-X^{\prime}\right\rVert_{2,\infty},\qquad\Delta_{A}=\left\lVert A-A^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty},\qquad\Delta_{\mathrm{seed}}=\left\lVert S_{q}-S_{q}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty},\qquad\Delta_{B}=\left\lVert B-B^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty},
``` |  | (88) |

where

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert H\right\rVert_{2,\infty}=\max_{v\in\bar{V}}\left\lVert h_{v}\right\rVert_{2},\qquad\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{\infty}=\max_{v}\sum_{u}\left\lvert A_{vu}\right\rvert.
``` |  | (89) |

For the gate input $`z_{uv}^{(l)}`$ at layer $`l`$, define the weighted
structural drift as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta_{Z}^{(l)}=\max_{v}\sum_{u}A^{\prime}_{vu}\left\lVert z_{uv}^{(l)}-z_{uv}^{\prime(l)}\right\rVert_{2},\qquad\Delta_{Z}=\max_{1\leq l\leq L}\Delta_{Z}^{(l)}.
``` |  | (90) |

The total augmented graph drift is defined as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}(G,G^{\prime};q)=\Delta_{X}+\Delta_{A}+\Delta_{\mathrm{seed}}+\Delta_{Z}+\Delta_{B}.
``` |  | (91) |

### E.2 Stability Assumptions

###### Assumption E.3 (Normalized adjacency).

For all considered graphs, $`A_{vu}\geq 0`$ and

|     |                                             |     |      |
|-----|---------------------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \sum_{u}A_{vu}=1,\qquad\forall v\in\bar{V}.
       ```                                          |     | (92) |

Therefore, $`\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{\infty}=1`$. This assumption
allows high-degree hubs to exist, but prevents single-layer propagation
from being unboundedly amplified by node degree.

###### Assumption E.4 (Trajectory-local boundedness).

For graph pairs $`G,G^{\prime}`$ on the training and inference
trajectories, there exist constants $`B_{l}`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert H^{(l)}(q,G)\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq B_{l},\qquad\left\lVert H^{(l)}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq B_{l},\qquad l=0,\dots,L.
``` |  | (93) |

###### Assumption E.5 (Locally Lipschitz modules).

In a neighborhood of the training trajectory, the $`l`$-th layer
satisfies

|     |                                                    |     |      |
|-----|----------------------------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \left\lVert W_{m}^{(l)}\right\rVert_{2}\leq M_{l},
       ```                                                 |     | (94) |

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert\operatorname{MLP}_{g}^{(l)}(z)-\operatorname{MLP}_{g}^{(l)}(z^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq L_{g,l}\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert_{2}.
``` |  | (95) |

The Lipschitz constant of PReLU is $`L_{\sigma}`$, and the Lipschitz
constant of LayerNorm with a numerical stabilizer in this trajectory
neighborhood is $`L_{\LN,l}`$.

###### Assumption E.6 (Local Lipschitzness of score head and projection).

The entity score head satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert s_{E}(q,G)-s_{E}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq L_{E}\left\lVert H(q,G)-H(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}.
``` |  | (96) |

Meanwhile, $`\left\lVert s_{E}(q,G)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq S_{E}`$,
and $`B`$ is row-normalized, so
$`\left\lVert B\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1`$.

### E.3 Stability of Soft Addressing and Initial Representation

###### Lemma E.7 (Softmax and pre-activation stability).

Let

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
p=\operatorname{softmax}(S/T_{0}),\qquad p^{\prime}=\operatorname{softmax}(S^{\prime}/T_{0}).
``` |  | (97) |

Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert p-p^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{T_{0}}\left\lVert S-S^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}.
``` |  | (98) |

Furthermore, let $`a_{v}=(p_{v}+\epsilon_{p})^{\eta}`$ and
$`a_{v}^{\prime}=(p_{v}^{\prime}+\epsilon_{p})^{\eta}`$, where
$`0<\eta\leq 1`$. Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert a-a^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{\eta\epsilon_{p}^{\eta-1}}{T_{0}}\left\lVert S-S^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}.
``` |  | (99) |

###### Proof.

The Jacobian of softmax is $`J(z)=\operatorname{diag}(p)-pp^{\top}`$.
For any row $`i`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sum_{j}\left\lvert J_{ij}(z)\right\rvert=2p_{i}(1-p_{i})\leq 1.
``` |  | (100) |

Thus, $`\left\lVert J(z)\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 1`$. By the
mean value theorem and setting $`z=S/T_{0}`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert p-p^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{T_{0}}\left\lVert S-S^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}.
``` |  | (101) |

The function $`t\mapsto(t+\epsilon_{p})^{\eta}`$ is Lipschitz on
$`[0,1]`$, with constant at most $`\eta\epsilon_{p}^{\eta-1}`$.
Composing the two inequalities gives the conclusion. ∎

###### Lemma E.8 (Initial node representation stability).

Let $`u_{q}=W_{q}\operatorname{Emb}(q)`$, and define

|     |                                       |     |       |
|-----|---------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       h_{v}^{(0)}=a_{v}(q)u_{q}+W_{x}x_{v}.
       ```                                    |     | (102) |

Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert H^{(0)}(q,G)-H^{(0)}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C_{\mathrm{init}}(\Delta_{\mathrm{seed}}+\Delta_{X}),
``` |  | (103) |

where

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
C_{\mathrm{init}}=\frac{\eta\epsilon_{p}^{\eta-1}}{T_{0}}\left\lVert u_{q}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert W_{x}\right\rVert_{2}.
``` |  | (104) |

###### Proof.

For any node $`v`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
h_{v}^{(0)}-h_{v}^{\prime(0)}=(a_{v}-a_{v}^{\prime})u_{q}+W_{x}(x_{v}-x_{v}^{\prime}).
``` |  | (105) |

Taking the norm and applying Lemma 4.8 gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert h_{v}^{(0)}-h_{v}^{\prime(0)}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{\eta\epsilon_{p}^{\eta-1}}{T_{0}}\left\lVert u_{q}\right\rVert_{2}\Delta_{\mathrm{seed}}+\left\lVert W_{x}\right\rVert_{2}\Delta_{X}.
``` |  | (106) |

Taking the maximum over $`v`$ gives the conclusion. ∎

### E.4 Single-layer Stability of Structurally Gated Propagation

###### Lemma E.9 (Boundedness and stability of structural gate).

The structural gate of layer $`l`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g_{uv}^{(l)}=1+\delta\tanh(\operatorname{MLP}_{g}^{(l)}(z_{uv}^{(l)})),
``` |  | (107) |

satisfies

|     |                                                             |     |       |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \left\lVert g_{uv}^{(l)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1+\delta,
       ```                                                          |     | (108) |

and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert g_{uv}^{(l)}-g_{uv}^{\prime(l)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\delta L_{g,l}\left\lVert z_{uv}^{(l)}-z_{uv}^{\prime(l)}\right\rVert_{2}.
``` |  | (109) |

###### Proof.

Since the range of $`\tanh`$ is contained in $`[-1,1]`$, the first
statement follows immediately. Moreover, because $`\tanh`$ is
1-Lipschitz and $`\operatorname{MLP}_{g}^{(l)}`$ is
$`L_{g,l}`$-Lipschitz in the trajectory neighborhood,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\left\lVert g_{uv}^{(l)}-g_{uv}^{\prime(l)}\right\rVert_{\infty}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\delta\left\lVert\operatorname{MLP}_{g}^{(l)}(z_{uv}^{(l)})-\operatorname{MLP}_{g}^{(l)}(z_{uv}^{\prime(l)})\right\rVert_{\infty}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\delta L_{g,l}\left\lVert z_{uv}^{(l)}-z_{uv}^{\prime(l)}\right\rVert_{2}.`$ |  | (110) |

∎

###### Lemma E.10 (Single-layer stability of structurally gated propagation).

Define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{l}=\left\lVert H^{(l)}(q,G)-H^{(l)}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}.
``` |  | (111) |

Under Assumptions 4.5–4.7, the $`l`$-th propagation layer satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{l}\leq\alpha_{l}D_{l-1}+\beta_{l}^{A}\Delta_{A}+\beta_{l}^{Z}\Delta_{Z}^{(l)},
``` |  | (112) |

where one can take

|     |                                                              |     |       |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \alpha_{l}=L_{\LN,l}\bigl(1+L_{\sigma}(1+\delta)M_{l}\bigr),
       ```                                                           |     | (113) |

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta_{l}^{A}=L_{\LN,l}L_{\sigma}(1+\delta)M_{l}B_{l-1},\qquad\beta_{l}^{Z}=L_{\LN,l}L_{\sigma}\delta L_{g,l}M_{l}B_{l-1}.
``` |  | (114) |

###### Proof.

Write

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
M_{v}=\sum_{u}A_{vu}g_{uv}\odot Wh_{u},\qquad M_{v}^{\prime}=\sum_{u}A^{\prime}_{vu}g^{\prime}_{uv}\odot Wh^{\prime}_{u},
``` |  | (115) |

where the layer index $`l`$ is omitted. Adding and subtracting
intermediate terms gives

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle M_{v}-M_{v}^{\prime}={}`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{u}A_{vu}g_{uv}\odot W(h_{u}-h^{\prime}_{u})`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\sum_{u}(A_{vu}-A^{\prime}_{vu})g_{uv}\odot Wh^{\prime}_{u}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\sum_{u}A^{\prime}_{vu}(g_{uv}-g^{\prime}_{uv})\odot Wh^{\prime}_{u}.`$ |  | (116) |

The first term is controlled by row-normalization,
$`\left\lVert g_{uv}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1+\delta`$, and
$`\left\lVert W\right\rVert_{2}\leq M_{l}`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert\sum_{u}A_{vu}g_{uv}\odot W(h_{u}-h^{\prime}_{u})\right\rVert_{2}\leq(1+\delta)M_{l}D_{l-1}.
``` |  | (117) |

The second term satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert\sum_{u}(A_{vu}-A^{\prime}_{vu})g_{uv}\odot Wh^{\prime}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}\leq(1+\delta)M_{l}B_{l-1}\sum_{u}\left\lvert A_{vu}-A^{\prime}_{vu}\right\rvert\leq(1+\delta)M_{l}B_{l-1}\Delta_{A}.
``` |  | (118) |

For the third term, by Lemma 4.10,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{u}A^{\prime}_{vu}(g_{uv}-g^{\prime}_{uv})\odot Wh^{\prime}_{u}\right\rVert_{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\delta L_{g,l}M_{l}B_{l-1}\sum_{u}A^{\prime}_{vu}\left\lVert z_{uv}^{(l)}-z_{uv}^{\prime(l)}\right\rVert_{2}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\delta L_{g,l}M_{l}B_{l-1}\Delta_{Z}^{(l)}.`$ |  | (119) |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert M_{v}-M_{v}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{2}\leq(1+\delta)M_{l}D_{l-1}+(1+\delta)M_{l}B_{l-1}\Delta_{A}+\delta L_{g,l}M_{l}B_{l-1}\Delta_{Z}^{(l)}.
``` |  | (120) |

By the $`L_{\sigma}`$-Lipschitz property of PReLU, the residual
structure, and the $`L_{\LN,l}`$-Lipschitz property of LayerNorm,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert h_{v}^{(l)}-h_{v}^{\prime(l)}\right\rVert_{2}\leq L_{\LN,l}\left(\left\lVert h_{v}^{(l-1)}-h_{v}^{\prime(l-1)}\right\rVert_{2}+L_{\sigma}\left\lVert M_{v}-M_{v}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{2}\right).
``` |  | (121) |

Taking the maximum over $`v`$ gives
Eq. ([112](#A5.E112 "Equation 112 ‣ Lemma E.10 (Single-layer stability of structurally gated propagation). ‣ E.4 Single-layer Stability of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ Appendix E Reader Stability under Dynamic Graph Evolution ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")).
∎

### E.5 Stability of Representations, Scores, and Retrieval Sets

###### Theorem E.11 (Local stability of structurally gated representations to augmented graph drift).

Under Assumptions 4.5–4.7, $`L`$-layer structurally gated propagation
satisfies

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle D_{L}\leq{}`$ | $`\displaystyle\left(\prod_{l=1}^{L}\alpha_{l}\right)D_{0}+\sum_{t=1}^{L}\left(\prod_{l=t+1}^{L}\alpha_{l}\right)\left(\beta_{t}^{A}\Delta_{A}+\beta_{t}^{Z}\Delta_{Z}^{(t)}\right).`$ |  | (122) |

Therefore, there exists a constant $`C_{H}>0`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert H^{(L)}(q,G)-H^{(L)}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C_{H}(\Delta_{X}+\Delta_{\mathrm{seed}}+\Delta_{A}+\Delta_{Z}).
``` |  | (123) |

###### Proof.

By Lemma 4.11, the recursion in
Eq. ([112](#A5.E112 "Equation 112 ‣ Lemma E.10 (Single-layer stability of structurally gated propagation). ‣ E.4 Single-layer Stability of Structurally Gated Propagation ‣ Appendix E Reader Stability under Dynamic Graph Evolution ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
holds. Unrolling the recursion layer by layer gives
Eq. ([122](#A5.E122 "Equation 122 ‣ Theorem E.11 (Local stability of structurally gated representations to augmented graph drift). ‣ E.5 Stability of Representations, Scores, and Retrieval Sets ‣ Appendix E Reader Stability under Dynamic Graph Evolution ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")).
By Lemma 4.9,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{0}\leq C_{\mathrm{init}}(\Delta_{X}+\Delta_{\mathrm{seed}}).
``` |  | (124) |

Substituting $`\Delta_{Z}^{(t)}\leq\Delta_{Z}`$ and merging all
layer-related constants into $`C_{H}`$ gives the conclusion. ∎

###### Theorem E.12 (Stability of context/schema dual channels).

If the two channels respectively satisfy

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert H_{\mathrm{ctx}}(q,G)-H_{\mathrm{ctx}}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C_{\mathrm{ctx}}\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}},
``` |  | (125) |

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert H_{\mathrm{sch}}(q,G)-H_{\mathrm{sch}}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C_{\mathrm{sch}}\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}},
``` |  | (126) |

then the additive fusion satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert H(q,G)-H(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq(C_{\mathrm{ctx}}+\left\lvert\beta_{\mathrm{sch}}\right\rvert C_{\mathrm{sch}})\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}.
``` |  | (127) |

If a normalized or gated convex fusion theoretical form is adopted,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
H_{\lambda}(q,G)=(1-\lambda)H_{\mathrm{ctx}}(q,G)+\lambda H_{\mathrm{sch}}(q,G),\qquad 0\leq\lambda\leq 1,
``` |  | (128) |

then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert H_{\lambda}(q,G)-H_{\lambda}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\bigl((1-\lambda)C_{\mathrm{ctx}}+\lambda C_{\mathrm{sch}}\bigr)\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}.
``` |  | (129) |

In particular, if $`C_{\mathrm{sch}}<C_{\mathrm{ctx}}`$, increasing
$`\lambda`$ decreases this worst-case stability upper bound.

###### Proof.

The additive fusion case follows directly from the triangle inequality:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\left\lVert H(G)-H(G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert H_{\mathrm{ctx}}(G)-H_{\mathrm{ctx}}(G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}+\left\lvert\beta_{\mathrm{sch}}\right\rvert\left\lVert H_{\mathrm{sch}}(G)-H_{\mathrm{sch}}(G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq(C_{\mathrm{ctx}}+\left\lvert\beta_{\mathrm{sch}}\right\rvert C_{\mathrm{sch}})\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}.`$ |  | (130) |

The convex fusion case is analogous:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\left\lVert H_{\lambda}(G)-H_{\lambda}(G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2,\infty}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq(1-\lambda)C_{\mathrm{ctx}}\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}+\lambda C_{\mathrm{sch}}\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}.`$ |  | (131) |

If $`C_{\mathrm{sch}}<C_{\mathrm{ctx}}`$, the right-hand side is
monotonically decreasing in $`\lambda`$. ∎

###### Remark E.13.

Equation
([127](#A5.E127 "Equation 127 ‣ Theorem E.12 (Stability of context/schema dual channels). ‣ E.5 Stability of Representations, Scores, and Retrieval Sets ‣ Appendix E Reader Stability under Dynamic Graph Evolution ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
does not claim that the schema prior necessarily reduces the worst-case
Lipschitz constant of additive fusion; its main theoretical role also
lies in risk decomposition and sample complexity reduction. If
additional gating, normalization, or regularization constraints are
adopted in the implementation, then
Eq. ([129](#A5.E129 "Equation 129 ‣ Theorem E.12 (Stability of context/schema dual channels). ‣ E.5 Stability of Representations, Scores, and Retrieval Sets ‣ Appendix E Reader Stability under Dynamic Graph Evolution ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
shows that the schema channel can also serve as a low-sensitivity
channel to reduce graph evolution drift.

###### Theorem E.14 (Stability of entity scores and document scores).

Under Assumption 4.7, there exist constants $`C_{E},C_{D}>0`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert s_{E}(q,G)-s_{E}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{E}\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}},
``` |  | (132) |

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G)-s_{D}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{D}\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}.
``` |  | (133) |

For additive fusion, one can take

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
C_{E}=L_{E}(C_{\mathrm{ctx}}+\left\lvert\beta_{\mathrm{sch}}\right\rvert C_{\mathrm{sch}}),\qquad C_{D}=C_{E}+S_{E}.
``` |  | (134) |

###### Proof.

The entity score upper bound follows from the Lipschitz property of the
score head and Theorem 4.13. For document projection, $`s_{D}=Bs_{E}`$,
and therefore

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
s_{D}(G)-s_{D}(G^{\prime})=B_{G}(s_{E}(G)-s_{E}(G^{\prime}))+(B_{G}-B_{G^{\prime}})s_{E}(G^{\prime}).
``` |  | (135) |

Taking the $`\ell_{\infty}`$ norm and using
$`\left\lVert B_{G}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1`$ gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert s_{D}(G)-s_{D}(G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert s_{E}(G)-s_{E}(G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert B_{G}-B_{G^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert s_{E}(G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}.
``` |  | (136) |

Using $`\left\lVert s_{E}(G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq S_{E}`$
and $`\Delta_{B}\leq\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}`$ gives the document score
stability. ∎

###### Theorem E.15 (Boundary stability of hard top-$`k`$).

Let $`s=s_{D}(q,G)`$ and $`s^{\prime}=s_{D}(q,G^{\prime})`$, and suppose

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert s-s^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\epsilon_{s}.
``` |  | (137) |

Let $`t_{k}=s_{(k)}`$ be the $`k`$-th largest score in $`s`$, and define
the boundary set

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{B}_{k,2\epsilon_{s}}(s)=\{d:\left\lvert s_{d}-t_{k}\right\rvert\leq 2\epsilon_{s}\}.
``` |  | (138) |

Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s)\triangle\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s^{\prime})\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{k,2\epsilon_{s}}(s),
``` |  | (139) |

and hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lvert\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s)\triangle\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert\mathcal{B}_{k,2\epsilon_{s}}(s)\right\rvert.
``` |  | (140) |

In particular, if $`s_{(k)}-s_{(k+1)}>2\epsilon_{s}`$, then
$`\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s)=\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s^{\prime})`$.

###### Proof.

Take any
$`i\in\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s)\setminus\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s^{\prime})`$.
Since $`i`$ drops out of the top-$`k`$, there exists
$`j\notin\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s)`$ such that
$`j\in\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s^{\prime})`$ and
$`s_{j}^{\prime}\geq s_{i}^{\prime}`$. By the perturbation bound,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
s_{j}+\epsilon_{s}\geq s_{j}^{\prime}\geq s_{i}^{\prime}\geq s_{i}-\epsilon_{s},
``` |  | (141) |

so $`s_{i}\leq s_{j}+2\epsilon_{s}\leq t_{k}+2\epsilon_{s}`$. Since
$`i\in\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s)`$, we have $`s_{i}\geq t_{k}`$, and hence
$`i\in\mathcal{B}_{k,2\epsilon_{s}}(s)`$. A symmetric argument for
$`j\in\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s^{\prime})\setminus\mathrm{Top}\text{-}k(s)`$
gives $`j\in\mathcal{B}_{k,2\epsilon_{s}}(s)`$. Therefore, the symmetric
difference is contained in the boundary set. If
$`s_{(k)}-s_{(k+1)}>2\epsilon_{s}`$, boundary exchange cannot occur, and
the top-$`k`$ set remains unchanged. ∎

###### Corollary E.16 (Top-$`k`$ boundary stability under graph evolution).

By Theorem 4.15, taking
$`\epsilon_{s}=C_{D}\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}(G,G^{\prime};q)`$ yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
P_{k}(q,G)\triangle P_{k}(q,G^{\prime})\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{k,2C_{D}\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}}(s_{D}(q,G)).
``` |  | (142) |

Therefore, the instability of hard top-$`k`$ is restricted to candidates
near the original score boundary.

###### Theorem E.17 (Stability of soft retrieval distribution).

Let

|     |                                                       |     |       |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \pi_{D}(q,G)=\operatorname{softmax}(s_{D}(q,G)/\tau).
       ```                                                    |     | (143) |

If
$`\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G)-s_{D}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\epsilon_{s}`$,
then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert\pi_{D}(q,G)-\pi_{D}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{1}\leq\frac{2}{\tau}\epsilon_{s}.
``` |  | (144) |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert\pi_{D}(q,G)-\pi_{D}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{1}\leq\frac{2C_{D}}{\tau}\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}(G,G^{\prime};q).
``` |  | (145) |

###### Proof.

The Jacobian of softmax is
$`J(z)=\operatorname{diag}(\pi)-\pi\pi^{\top}`$. For any perturbation
$`r`$,

|     |                                      |     |       |
|-----|--------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       J(z)r=\pi\odot(r-\mathbb{E}_{\pi}r).
       ```                                   |     | (146) |

If $`\left\lVert r\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1`$, then
$`\left\lvert r_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\pi}r\right\rvert\leq 2`$, so

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert J(z)r\right\rVert_{1}\leq\sum_{i}\pi_{i}\left\lvert r_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\pi}r\right\rvert\leq 2.
``` |  | (147) |

Thus, the $`\ell_{\infty}\to\ell_{1}`$ Lipschitz constant of softmax is
at most $`2`$. Since the input is $`s_{D}/\tau`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert\pi_{D}(s)-\pi_{D}(s^{\prime})\right\rVert_{1}\leq\frac{2}{\tau}\left\lVert s-s^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}.
``` |  | (148) |

Substituting Theorem 4.15 gives the conclusion. ∎

###### Theorem E.18 (High-probability graph evolution stability).

If the writer’s single-round graph update satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}[\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}(G,G^{\prime};q)>\epsilon]\leq\delta,
``` |  | (149) |

then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}\left[\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G)-s_{D}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}>C_{D}\epsilon\right]\leq\delta.
``` |  | (150) |

If
$`\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}(G,G^{\prime};q)]\leq\bar{\epsilon}`$,
then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G)-s_{D}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}\right]\leq C_{D}\bar{\epsilon}.
``` |  | (151) |

###### Proof.

By Theorem 4.15, for any graph pair, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G)-s_{D}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{D}\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}(G,G^{\prime};q).
``` |  | (152) |

Therefore, the event
$`\{\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G)-s_{D}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}>C_{D}\epsilon\}`$
implies the event
$`\{\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}(G,G^{\prime};q)>\epsilon\}`$, so the
probability upper bound follows immediately. The expectation conclusion
follows by taking expectations on both sides of the deterministic
inequality. ∎

### E.6 Local Influence Cone

###### Proposition 8 (Influence cone of local graph updates).

Suppose the writer only changes nodes, edges, anchors, or attributes on
a primitive set $`\mathcal{U}`$. Suppose that the structural gate input
$`z_{uv}^{(l)}`$, except for the graph-level summary, only depends on a
local neighborhood of radius $`r_{z}`$, and that $`G`$ and
$`G^{\prime}`$ are exactly the same outside
$`\mathcal{N}_{L+r_{z}}(\mathcal{U})`$. If graph-level summary drift is
ignored, then for any

|     |                                            |     |       |
|-----|--------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       v\notin\mathcal{N}_{L+r_{z}}(\mathcal{U}),
       ```                                         |     | (153) |

we have

|     |                                             |     |       |
|-----|---------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       h_{v}^{(L)}(q,G)=h_{v}^{(L)}(q,G^{\prime}).
       ```                                          |     | (154) |

If the graph-level summary drift is
$`\rho_{g}=\left\lVert r_{G}-r_{G^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}`$, then
there exists a constant $`C_{g}`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert h_{v}^{(L)}(q,G)-h_{v}^{(L)}(q,G^{\prime})\right\rVert_{2}\leq C_{g}\rho_{g}.
``` |  | (155) |

###### Proof.

First consider the case without graph-level summary drift. We induct on
the layer index $`l`$. For $`l=0`$, if
$`v\notin\mathcal{N}_{L+r_{z}}(\mathcal{U})`$, then its node features,
presence bit, and seed score are all identical, so
$`h_{v}^{(0)}(G)=h_{v}^{(0)}(G^{\prime})`$. Suppose that at layer
$`l-1`$, all nodes whose distance from $`\mathcal{U}`$ exceeds
$`L+r_{z}-(l-1)`$ have identical representations. If
$`v\notin\mathcal{N}_{L+r_{z}-l}(\mathcal{U})`$, then all its one-hop
neighbors $`u`$ do not belong to
$`\mathcal{N}_{L+r_{z}-(l-1)}(\mathcal{U})`$; by the induction
hypothesis, $`h_{u}^{(l-1)}(G)=h_{u}^{(l-1)}(G^{\prime})`$. Meanwhile,
the radius-$`r_{z}`$ local structural contexts of all relevant edges are
also identical, so the gate, message multiset, and aggregation result
are identical, and hence $`h_{v}^{(l)}(G)=h_{v}^{(l)}(G^{\prime})`$.
Taking $`l=L`$ gives the first conclusion. If graph-level summary drift
exists, then the gate input has an additional uniform perturbation term
$`\rho_{g}`$, and a $`C_{g}\rho_{g}`$-type upper bound follows from
Lemma 4.10 and the recursion in Theorem 4.12. ∎

## Appendix F Theoretical Motivation of the Self-evolving Writer-Reader Loop

### F.1 Joint Memory Utility

The reader-aware writer reward can consist of evidence coverage,
precision, deducibility, and answer utility. Abstractly, define the
joint memory utility as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{J}(\theta,\phi)=\mathbb{E}_{(q,D,D^{+},y)}\left[U\left(R_{\phi}(q,W_{\theta}(q,D),D),D^{+},y\right)\right],
``` |  | (156) |

where $`U`$ can be taken as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
U=\frac{\alpha r_{\mathrm{rec}}+\beta r_{\mathrm{pre}}+\gamma r_{\mathrm{ded}}}{\alpha+\beta+\gamma}-\lambda_{\mathrm{rep}}\rho_{\mathrm{rep}}+\lambda_{\mathrm{fmt}}r_{\mathrm{fmt}},
``` |  | (157) |

or an extended form including answer-level reward. This definition
places the writer’s graph construction quality and the reader’s graph
reading ability under the same objective.

### F.2 Approximate Coordinate Improvement

###### Theorem F.1 (The self-evolution process is approximate coordinate improvement on joint utility).

Suppose that the writer update at round $`r`$ satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r+1)},\phi^{(r)})\geq\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r)},\phi^{(r)})+\Delta_{W}^{(r)}-\epsilon_{W}^{(r)},
``` |  | (158) |

and the reader update satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r+1)},\phi^{(r+1)})\geq\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r+1)},\phi^{(r)})+\Delta_{R}^{(r)}-\epsilon_{R}^{(r)}.
``` |  | (159) |

Then one full round of writer-reader self-evolution satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r+1)},\phi^{(r+1)})-\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r)},\phi^{(r)})\geq\Delta_{W}^{(r)}+\Delta_{R}^{(r)}-\epsilon_{W}^{(r)}-\epsilon_{R}^{(r)}.
``` |  | (160) |

Therefore, as long as
$`\Delta_{W}^{(r)}+\Delta_{R}^{(r)}>\epsilon_{W}^{(r)}+\epsilon_{R}^{(r)}`$,
the joint memory utility improves in that round.

###### Proof.

By telescoping decomposition,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r+1)},\phi^{(r+1)})-\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r)},\phi^{(r)})`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle={}`$ | $`\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r+1)},\phi^{(r+1)})-\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r+1)},\phi^{(r)})\right]+\left[\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r+1)},\phi^{(r)})-\mathcal{J}(\theta^{(r)},\phi^{(r)})\right].`$ |  | (161) |

Substituting
Eq. ([158](#A6.E158 "Equation 158 ‣ Theorem F.1 (The self-evolution process is approximate coordinate improvement on joint utility). ‣ F.2 Approximate Coordinate Improvement ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Motivation of the Self-evolving Writer-Reader Loop ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
and
Eq. ([159](#A6.E159 "Equation 159 ‣ Theorem F.1 (The self-evolution process is approximate coordinate improvement on joint utility). ‣ F.2 Approximate Coordinate Improvement ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Motivation of the Self-evolving Writer-Reader Loop ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")),
respectively, gives the conclusion. ∎

### F.3 Reader Reward Bias and Calibration Benefit

###### Definition F.2 (True utility and reader surrogate reward).

Let $`U^{\star}(G)`$ denote the true utility of graph memory $`G`$ with
respect to the downstream task, and let $`\widehat{U}_{\phi}(G)`$ denote
the surrogate reward constructed from the readout result of reader
$`R_{\phi}`$. We say that the reader reward bias is at most
$`\epsilon_{\phi}`$ if, for all considered graphs $`G`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lvert\widehat{U}_{\phi}(G)-U^{\star}(G)\right\rvert\leq\epsilon_{\phi}.
``` |  | (162) |

###### Theorem F.3 (Surrogate reward improvement to true utility improvement).

If the reader reward bias is at most $`\epsilon_{\phi}`$, and the writer
update improves the surrogate reward by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{U}_{\phi}(G_{\theta^{\prime}})-\widehat{U}_{\phi}(G_{\theta})\geq\Delta,
``` |  | (163) |

then the true utility satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
U^{\star}(G_{\theta^{\prime}})-U^{\star}(G_{\theta})\geq\Delta-2\epsilon_{\phi}.
``` |  | (164) |

###### Proof.

By the bias assumption,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
U^{\star}(G_{\theta^{\prime}})\geq\widehat{U}_{\phi}(G_{\theta^{\prime}})-\epsilon_{\phi},\qquad U^{\star}(G_{\theta})\leq\widehat{U}_{\phi}(G_{\theta})+\epsilon_{\phi}.
``` |  | (165) |

Subtracting the two inequalities gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
U^{\star}(G_{\theta^{\prime}})-U^{\star}(G_{\theta})\geq\widehat{U}_{\phi}(G_{\theta^{\prime}})-\widehat{U}_{\phi}(G_{\theta})-2\epsilon_{\phi}\geq\Delta-2\epsilon_{\phi}.
``` |  | (166) |

∎

###### Corollary F.4 (Reader calibration reduces writer optimization bias).

If the reader is calibrated from $`\phi`$ to $`\phi^{\prime}`$ and
reduces the reward bias from $`\epsilon_{\phi}`$ to
$`\epsilon_{\phi^{\prime}}`$, where
$`\epsilon_{\phi^{\prime}}<\epsilon_{\phi}`$, then for the same
surrogate reward improvement $`\Delta`$, the lower bound on true utility
improvement increases by

|     |                                              |     |       |
|-----|----------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       2(\epsilon_{\phi}-\epsilon_{\phi^{\prime}}).
       ```                                           |     | (167) |

###### Proof.

By Theorem 5.3, the true utility improvement lower bound before
calibration is $`\Delta-2\epsilon_{\phi}`$, and after calibration it is
$`\Delta-2\epsilon_{\phi^{\prime}}`$. Subtracting the two gives the
result. ∎

### F.4 Irreducible Bottlenecks of Single-sided Updates

###### Proposition 9 (Lower-bound bottlenecks of single-sided updates).

Assume that the overall error can be decomposed as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}(\theta,\phi)=\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{write}}(\theta)+\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{read}}(\phi;\theta)+\epsilon_{\mathrm{int}}(\theta,\phi),
``` |  | (168) |

where all terms are nonnegative. If only the reader is updated, i.e.,
$`\phi\mapsto\phi^{\prime}`$ while $`\theta`$ is fixed, then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\geq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{write}}(\theta).
``` |  | (169) |

If only the writer is updated, i.e., $`\theta\mapsto\theta^{\prime}`$
while $`\phi`$ is fixed, then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}(\theta^{\prime},\phi)\geq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{read}}(\phi;\theta^{\prime}).
``` |  | (170) |

Therefore, reader-only updates cannot compensate for evidence chains
that the writer has not written; writer-only updates cannot guarantee
that a fixed reader can read out the evidence structures in the new
graph distribution.

###### Proof.

By the decomposition in
Eq. ([168](#A6.E168 "Equation 168 ‣ Proposition 9 (Lower-bound bottlenecks of single-sided updates). ‣ F.4 Irreducible Bottlenecks of Single-sided Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Motivation of the Self-evolving Writer-Reader Loop ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory"))
and the nonnegativity of all terms,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}(\theta,\phi^{\prime})=\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{write}}(\theta)+\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{read}}(\phi^{\prime};\theta)+\epsilon_{\mathrm{int}}(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\geq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{write}}(\theta).
``` |  | (171) |

The second inequality is analogous. ∎

### F.5 Stability of Closed-loop Graph Evolution and Parameter Updates

###### Theorem F.5 (Score drift control under multi-round self-evolution).

Let the graph at round $`r`$ be $`G^{(r)}`$, and the reader parameter be
$`\phi^{(r)}`$. If single-step graph stability satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G^{(r+1)};\phi^{(r)})-s_{D}(q,G^{(r)};\phi^{(r)})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{D}\Delta_{r},
``` |  | (172) |

where $`\Delta_{r}=\Delta_{\mathrm{aug}}(G^{(r)},G^{(r+1)};q)`$; and if
the score is locally Lipschitz with respect to the parameter:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G;\phi)-s_{D}(q,G;\phi^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{\phi}\left\lVert\phi-\phi^{\prime}\right\rVert_{2},
``` |  | (173) |

then

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G^{(T)};\phi^{(T)})-s_{D}(q,G^{(0)};\phi^{(0)})\right\rVert_{\infty}`$ |  | (174) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r=0}^{T-1}\left(C_{D}\Delta_{r}+C_{\phi}\left\lVert\phi^{(r+1)}-\phi^{(r)}\right\rVert_{2}\right).`$ |  |  |

###### Proof.

For each $`r`$, adding and subtracting the intermediate term
$`s_{D}(q,G^{(r+1)};\phi^{(r)})`$ gives

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G^{(r+1)};\phi^{(r+1)})-s_{D}(q,G^{(r)};\phi^{(r)})\right\rVert_{\infty}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G^{(r+1)};\phi^{(r+1)})-s_{D}(q,G^{(r+1)};\phi^{(r)})\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G^{(r+1)};\phi^{(r)})-s_{D}(q,G^{(r)};\phi^{(r)})\right\rVert_{\infty}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\phi}\left\lVert\phi^{(r+1)}-\phi^{(r)}\right\rVert_{2}+C_{D}\Delta_{r}.`$ |  | (175) |

Summing over $`r=0,\dots,T-1`$ and using the triangle inequality gives
the conclusion. ∎

###### Corollary F.6 (High-probability multi-round stability).

If $`\mathbb{P}[\Delta_{r}>\epsilon_{r}]\leq\delta_{r}`$, then with
probability at least $`1-\sum_{r=0}^{T-1}\delta_{r}`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left\lVert s_{D}(q,G^{(T)};\phi^{(T)})-s_{D}(q,G^{(0)};\phi^{(0)})\right\rVert_{\infty}`$ |  | (176) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r=0}^{T-1}\left(C_{D}\epsilon_{r}+C_{\phi}\left\lVert\phi^{(r+1)}-\phi^{(r)}\right\rVert_{2}\right).`$ |  |  |

###### Proof.

By a union bound, the event $`\{\forall r,\Delta_{r}\leq\epsilon_{r}\}`$
holds with probability at least $`1-\sum_{r}\delta_{r}`$. Applying
Theorem 5.7 on this event gives the conclusion. ∎

## Appendix G Analysis of the Memory Writer

This section further analyzes the memory writer while keeping the memory
reader fixed. The experiments mainly use HotpotQA and MuSiQue. To
further examine domain transfer capability, we also evaluate the trained
writing policy on GRBench-Amazon, HaluMem-Medium, and
LongMemEval-Oracle ([Wu et al., 2025a](#bib.bib47); [Chen et al.,
2025a](#bib.bib51)). We primarily report Precision, Recall, and
Deducible: the first two measure whether the text retrieved by the
reader covers the gold supporting contexts, while Deducible is
determined by a judge as to whether the standard answer can be inferred
from the retrieved context, thus more directly reflecting whether the
graph memory is usable for reasoning.

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
| Methods | Prec.$`\uparrow`$ | Recall$`\uparrow`$ | Deducible$`\uparrow`$ |
| GFM-pretrained-only | 0.838 | 0.818 | 0.510 |
| GFM-finetuned | 0.824 | 0.813 | 0.512 |
| RL-Recall | 0.889 | 0.835 | 0.502 |
| RL-F1 | 0.839 | 0.881 | 0.497 |
| RL-Deduce | 0.861 | 0.892 | 0.517 |
| RL-Hybrid | 0.902 | 0.917 | 0.522 |
| Hybrid + frozen answer API | 0.832 | 0.874 | 0.526 |

Table 5: Training results for the memory writer. GFM-pretrained-only
refers to using rewards fed back only by the pretrained memory reader,
while GFM-finetuned further refers to using the fine-tuned memory
reader.

Table [5](#A7.T5 "Table 5 ‣ Appendix G Analysis of the Memory Writer ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
shows that different rewards have different preferences. Overall,
RL-Hybrid achieves the best overall results, indicating that hybrid
rewards can simultaneously constrain the selectivity and coverage of
graph writing. Hybrid + frozen answer API achieves the highest Deducible
but slightly lower retrieval Precision/Recall, suggesting that
answer-side feedback helps improve reasoning usability, but may also
make the writer more conservatively inclined to write evidence that
directly supports the answer.
Table [6](#A7.T6 "Table 6 ‣ Appendix G Analysis of the Memory Writer ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
shows that a writer learned on HotpotQA/MuSiQue can transfer to GRBench,
HaluMem, and LongMemEval, but continued training on the target domain
still brings significant improvements. This indicates that the memory
structure in agent memory scenarios is not entirely similar to that in
traditional multi-hop QA, so target-domain feedback remains crucial.
Table [7](#A7.T7 "Table 7 ‣ Appendix G Analysis of the Memory Writer ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
further shows that the writing protocol and interaction budget affect
the trade-off between coverage and noise. Relaxing the tight prompt can
improve Recall, but reduces Deducible. Increasing the number of
interaction steps can help complete cross-document bridging paths,
whereas an overly large interaction budget or a loose writing protocol
introduces redundant or noisy memory entries and can reduce downstream
retrieval quality. More detailed reader-side sensitivity, training
stability, and regularization analyses are provided in
Appendix [A](#A1 "Appendix A Additional Analysis of the Memory Writer ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory").

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
| Settings | Prec.$`\uparrow`$ | Recall$`\uparrow`$ | Deducible$`\uparrow`$ |
| Base$`\rightarrow`$GRBench | 0.575 | 0.609 | 0.411 |
| GRBench train$`\rightarrow`$val | 0.794 | 0.833 | 0.596 |
| Base$`\rightarrow`$HaluMem | 0.230 | 0.448 | 0.299 |
| HaluMem train$`\rightarrow`$val | 0.312 | 0.708 | 0.438 |
| Base$`\rightarrow`$LongMemEval | 0.232 | 0.376 | 0.475 |
| LongMem train$`\rightarrow`$LongMemEval | 0.377 | 0.439 | 0.531 |

Table 6: Cross-dataset memory writing results.
“Base$`\rightarrow`$Target” indicates direct evaluation on the target
domain after training on the HotpotQA/MuSiQue base; “Target
train$`\rightarrow`$val” indicates training and validation on the target
domain.

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
| Settings | Prec.$`\uparrow`$ | Recall$`\uparrow`$ | Deducible$`\uparrow`$ |
| Tight=True | 0.836 | 0.806 | 0.515 |
| Tight=False | 0.845 | 0.851 | 0.506 |
| Iterative, 12 turns, tight | 0.852 | 0.829 | 0.516 |
| Iterative, 20 turns, tight | 0.835 | 0.881 | 0.522 |
| Iterative, 24 turns, loose | 0.863 | 0.826 | 0.531 |

Table 7: Ablation of writing protocols and interaction budgets.
Tight=True indicates that the writer performs a single-round graph write
under a stricter evidence budget, meaning the reader exposes only fewer,
higher-confidence candidate pieces of evidence to the writer;
Tight=False indicates that this evidence budget is relaxed, allowing the
writer to access a broader candidate context. Iterative indicates that
multi-round interactive writer-reader writing is enabled: the writer
first writes the initial graph memory, the reader then returns retrieval
feedback based on the current graph, and the writer continues to
supplement or revise the graph structure. Here, 12/20/24 turns indicates
the maximum number of interaction rounds allowed, and tight/loose
indicates that strict or relaxed evidence budget constraints are still
used during this multi-round interaction process.

## Appendix H Ablation Study of the Memory Reader

We conduct ablation studies to isolate the contribution of each major
component in the memory reader. All variants use the same
writer-produced graph memory and the same retrieval budget unless the
ablated component directly changes the retrieval mechanism. The
ablations are organized around four questions: (1) whether structured
query planning and global soft addressing are necessary for recovering
evidence from fragmented cues; (2) whether structurally gated
propagation improves over uniform graph propagation; (3) whether
cross-graph structural priors and target-graph calibration are both
needed for evolving graph memory; and (4) whether reader training and
entity-to-document projection are important for converting entity-level
activation into document-level retrieval.

Table [8](#A8.T8 "Table 8 ‣ Appendix H Ablation Study of the Memory Reader ‣ SAGE: A Self-Evolving Agentic Graph-Memory Engine for Structure-AwareAssociative Memory")
summarizes the results. The first group evaluates how the reader handles
fragmented cues. Removing structured query planning, alias/constraint
cues, or global soft addressing forces the reader to rely more heavily
on surface-level query matches or a small number of anchor entities,
directly testing whether the complete evidence chain can still be
recovered when distant bridge nodes are not initially activated. The
second group studies whether the reader uses graph structure in a
learned and selective way. Removing structural gates or replacing them
with uniform message passing tests whether treating hub edges, bridge
edges, redundant edges, and noisy shortcuts similarly harms retrieval.
The third group examines the context–schema decomposition: the schema
channel captures transferable structural reading patterns, while the
context channel adapts the reader to the current writer-produced graph.
````
